Even when my wife tries to get me to enjoy things we used to do on Sundays, or when something goes well at work, I just don’t feel much. It’s like I know I should feel good, but I don’t.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a loss of pleasure or interest in activities that you used to enjoy, like the things you mentioned doing on Sundays with your wife?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s probably the main thing I’ve noticed. The Sunday stuff with my wife used to feel good, like something I looked forward to, but now I just kind of go through it. And even if something goes well at work, I know it’s supposed to feel satisfying, but I don’t really feel happy or connected to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, nearly every day, even when nothing particularly bad is happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say sad most of the day, nearly every day. It’s more like I feel flat or kind of empty. I don’t always notice it as sadness exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel fatigued or that you’ve lost your energy, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really noticed that specifically. I mean, I get tired like anyone does, but I can’t say I’ve felt unusually drained when I haven’t done much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty concentrating or thinking clearly, even when you're not particularly stressed or tired?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I keep thinking I can’t trust my own judgment anymore. Like if I miss one small detail on a schedule or a machine setting, somebody could get hurt because of me. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower than usual, and I wouldn’t say I’m especially fidgety or unable to sit still.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way—like you don’t experience happiness or closeness anymore?\n\nPatient: Since the retrofit accident… so about a year and seven months now. It’s been pretty constant since then.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've completely lost interest in the activities you used to enjoy on Sundays with your wife, or do you still find some value or benefit in doing them even if you don’t feel happiness?\n\nPatient: I still do them sometimes because I know they matter to my wife, and I guess there’s some value in keeping the routine. I don’t always notice it as sadness exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel fatigued or that you’ve lost your energy, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really noticed that specifically. I mean, I get tired like anyone does, but I can’t say I’ve felt unusually drained when I haven’t done much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty concentrating or thinking clearly, even when you're not particularly stressed or tired?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have moments where I lose my train of thought like anyone, but I haven’t noticed a clear problem with concentrating when I’m not stressed or tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worthless or guilty, even when there isn’t a clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not exactly worthless, but guilty, I guess. I keep thinking I can’t trust my own judgment anymore. Like if I miss one small detail on a schedule or a machine setting, somebody could get hurt because of me. It’s hard to shake that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or alternatively, feeling restless or fidgety, as if you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower than usual, and I wouldn’t say I’m especially fidgety or unable to sit still.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way—like you don’t experience happiness or closeness anymore?\n\nPatient: Since the retrofit accident… so about a year and seven months now. It’s been pretty constant since then.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've completely lost interest in the activities you used to enjoy on Sundays with your wife, or do you still find some value or benefit in doing them even if you don’t feel happiness?\n\nPatient: I still do them sometimes because I know they matter to my wife, and I guess there’s some value in keeping the routine. But I don’t really feel happy from it anymore. Even when it should be nice, or when something goes well at work, I don’t feel that real happiness or closeness like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in nearly all activities, not just the ones you used to do on Sundays?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s broader than just Sundays. I still do things when I have to, or because they matter to my wife, but I don’t really get pleasure from them. Even if something goes well at work, I don’t feel happy the way I used to. It’s like that feeling just doesn’t really come.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your lack of pleasure or interest in activities has been present most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s there most of the day, pretty much every day. I can go through the motions, but I don’t feel real happiness or closeness anymore—even when my wife tries to get me into our old Sunday routines, or when something goes well at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight over the past several months—like eating much less or much more than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed a big change there. My appetite might go up and down a little, but I’m not aware of any significant weight loss or gain.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your sleep—like trouble falling asleep, waking up frequently during the night, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep has been bad. Most nights I’m lying there replaying the plant in my head, and if I do fall asleep, I’ll jolt awake from dreams about the line. That’s probably the main thing I’ve noticed. The Sunday stuff with my wife used to feel good, like something I looked forward to, but now I just kind of go through it. And even if something goes well at work, I know it’s supposed to feel satisfying, but I don’t really feel happy or connected to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, nearly every day, even when nothing particularly bad is happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say sad most of the day, nearly every day. It’s more like I feel flat or kind of empty. I don’t always notice it as sadness exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel fatigued or that you’ve lost your energy, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really noticed that specifically. I mean, I get tired like anyone does, but I can’t say I’ve felt unusually drained when I haven’t done much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty concentrating or thinking clearly, even when you're not particularly stressed or tired?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have moments where I lose my train of thought like anyone, but I haven’t noticed a clear problem with concentrating when I’m not stressed or tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worthless or guilty, even when there isn’t a clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not exactly worthless, but guilty, I guess. I keep thinking I can’t trust my own judgment anymore. Like if I miss one small detail on a schedule or a machine setting, somebody could get hurt because of me. It’s hard to shake that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or alternatively, feeling restless or fidgety, as if you can’t sit still?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t noticed myself moving slower than usual, and I wouldn’t say I’m especially fidgety or unable to sit still.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way—like you don’t experience happiness or closeness anymore?\n\nPatient: Since the retrofit accident… so about a year and seven months now. It’s been pretty constant since then.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've completely lost interest in the activities you used to enjoy on Sundays with your wife, or do you still find some value or benefit in doing them even if you don’t feel happiness?\n\nPatient: I still do them sometimes because I know they matter to my wife, and I guess there’s some value in keeping the routine. But I don’t really feel happy from it anymore. Even when it should be nice, or when something goes well at work, I don’t feel that real happiness or closeness like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in nearly all activities, not just the ones you used to do on Sundays?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s broader than just Sundays. I still do things when I have to, or because they matter to my wife, but I don’t really get pleasure from them. Even if something goes well at work, I don’t feel happy the way I used to. It’s like that feeling just doesn’t really come.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your lack of pleasure or interest in activities has been present most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s there most of the day, pretty much every day. I can go through the motions, but I don’t feel real happiness or closeness anymore—even when my wife tries to get me into our old Sunday routines, or when something goes well at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight over the past several months—like eating much less or much more than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed a big change there. My appetite might go up and down a little, but I’m not aware of any significant weight loss or gain.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your sleep—like trouble falling asleep, waking up frequently during the night, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep has been bad. Most nights I’m lying there replaying the plant in my head, and if I do fall asleep, I’ll jolt awake from dreams about the line. A lot of nights I’m up around 3 or 4 in the morning, and it feels like I never really rested.\n\nDoctor: Have you had episodes like this before, where you didn’t feel happiness or closeness, or is this the first time you’ve felt this way?\n\nPatient: This is the first time it’s felt like this. I mean, I’ve had rough periods before, but not where I just don’t feel real happiness or closeness. Even the things that used to matter—like Sundays with my wife, or doing well at work—just don’t really reach me now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

That’s probably the main thing I’ve noticed. The Sunday stuff with my wife used to feel good, like something I looked forward to, but now I just kind of go through it. And even if something goes well at work, I know it’s supposed to feel satisfying, but I don’t really feel happy or connected to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or sad most of the day, nearly every day, even when nothing particularly bad is happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say sad most of the day, nearly every day. It’s more like I feel flat or kind of empty. I don’t always notice it as sadness exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel fatigued or that you’ve lost your energy, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really noticed that specifically. I mean, I get tired like anyone does, but I can’t say I’ve felt unusually drained when I haven’t done much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty concentrating or thinking clearly, even when you're not particularly stressed or tired?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have moments where I lose my train of thought like anyone, but I haven’t noticed a clear problem with concentrating when I’m not stressed or tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worthless or guilty, even when there isn’t a clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not exactly worthless, but guilty, I guess.
